cua-driver-rs-v0.47.10cua-driver-rs: v0.47.10 Prerelease2026-11-11T12:05:05Z

Cua Driver 0.47.10

Summary

This release includes 1 fix.

Fixes

  • never remove XI2 master pairs; pool them instead. (#7358) reported by @will-bogusz.

Contributors

Thanks to @will-bogusz for contributing to this release.

Install

macOS and Linux:

/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://cua.ai/driver/install.sh)"

Windows:

irm https://cua.ai/driver/install.ps1 | iex

Cua Driver ships universal macOS binaries, x86_64 and arm64 Windows builds, and Linux preview builds. Release assets also include tag-pinned installer and uninstaller scripts.

Why GitHub says “Pre-release”

GitHub's label is used only to keep this monorepo's repository-wide “Latest” pointer from switching between independently released products. A plain Cua Driver SemVer is a stable release; npm and PyPI publish it on their normal stable channels, and the installers resolve the versioned cua-driver-rs-v* releases directly.

Cua Driver 0.47.7

Summary

This release includes 1 fix.

Fixes

  • hover background Hyprland apps through the agent seat. (#7319)

Contributors

This release contains maintainer changes only.

Install

macOS and Linux:

/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://cua.ai/driver/install.sh)"

Windows:

irm https://cua.ai/driver/install.ps1 | iex

Cua Driver ships universal macOS binaries, x86_64 and arm64 Windows builds, and Linux preview builds. Release assets also include tag-pinned installer and uninstaller scripts.

Why GitHub says “Pre-release”

GitHub's label is used only to keep this monorepo's repository-wide “Latest” pointer from switching between independently released products. A plain Cua Driver SemVer is a stable release; npm and PyPI publish it on their normal stable channels, and the installers resolve the versioned cua-driver-rs-v* releases directly.

tirth8205/code-review-graph ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v2.4.0 Stable2026-11-11T13:59:13Z

[2.4.0] - 2026-11-10

Two new platforms, MiMo Code and DeepSeek Harness, so install now supports 18. HTML, XSLT, Liquid, JSON and plain YAML files are indexed, and so are .cjs and .kts files, which every build used to skip. The graph links calls, imports and tests more carefully: a call it could not check no longer looks like a real link, every call edge says how sure it is, calls to inherited methods and imports of workspace packages are followed, and tests_for finds Java, Ruby and PHP tests. The graph schema moves from 13 to 15. Several new settings are off unless you turn them on. Read Upgrade notes at the end of this section before you upgrade.

Added

  • DeepSeek Harness is a supported platform (#1100, #1101, thanks @sumingwang233). install --platform dsh adds one insert entry for DSH's own MCP client to ~/.dsh/cordis.patch.yml (or $DSH_HOME/cordis.patch.yml), which every DSH profile reads, and the graph block to AGENTS.md. Comments, !!js values and other entries in the file are kept byte for byte, and uninstall takes the entry out again. Plain install picks DSH up when its home folder exists.
  • HTML pages (.html, .htm) are indexed (#521). They used to give nothing at all. Inline scripts are parsed as JavaScript or TypeScript with the page's own line numbers, and a <script src> becomes an import of the local file, so importers_of lists the pages that load a script. An onclick that calls a function on the page becomes a call. A div, section or article with an id becomes a new DocSection node, which you can find by its anchor or its first heading. The body text is not indexed. Style blocks and heading anchors stay out, and pages over 2 MB keep only their file node. Run build to pick up pages in a graph you already have.
  • XSLT stylesheets (.xsl, .xslt) are indexed (#1052). Each template and xsl:function becomes a Function node, named by its name or written as match=para mode=toc. xsl:import and xsl:include become imports, and xsl:call-template becomes a call. xsl:apply-templates is chosen from the input document when the stylesheet runs, so it becomes a call only where its select names a step or its mode narrows the choice. Where neither does, no edge is added and dynamic_apply_count on the template says so. A template with a match, named or not, is never reported as dead code. Run build to pick up stylesheets in a graph you already have.
  • CI now installs every direct dependency at its declared minimum, on each supported Python, and runs the test suite there. tests/test_dependency_floors.py keeps pyproject.toml and the pinned list in step in both directions. (#896)
  • A Dockerfile that works with Docker and Podman, so the tool can run from a container. It installs this source, includes Git, and runs as a non-root user. Mount a repository at /workspace; the graph stays in that repository. It stores /workspace paths, so use the container for later commands or rebuild on the host. No image is published. See the README. (#1037)
  • Liquid templates (.liquid) are indexed. A template gets Variable nodes for assign and Output nodes for {{ ... }}, and render and include link to the snippets/<name>.liquid file, so importers_of answers who renders a snippet and it counts towards impact. raw, comment, doc, schema, javascript and stylesheet blocks, # comments and quoted text are not read as code. Names hold no line number, so an edit above them does not rename them, and a repeated name is one node that lists every line. Output nodes are found by search only with kind="Output": on Shopify's Dawn theme they otherwise took all ten top hits for "product". (#878, #348)
  • get_impact_radius_tool(detail_level="compact") and code-review-graph impact --detail-level compact return the blast radius as ranked repository-relative file paths and counts, with no node or edge records. max_files (default 25, at most 200) limits each file list without shrinking the traversal, and changed files the graph has no nodes for are listed in unmatched_files. The CLI can also ask for --detail-level minimal.
  • build --enrich and update --enrich resolve Python method calls on a local variable with Jedi, from the enrichment extra (PR #823). For s = make() then s.authenticate(), the CALLS edge to the bare name authenticate then names svc.py::OldService.authenticate, so callers_of and the impact radius find the call. Jedi was a declared extra that no build ever ran. It is off unless you ask for it, with the flag or CRG_ENABLE_ENRICHMENT=1 (for hooks, the watcher and MCP builds), because it is slow: on an 88-file Python repository a full build went from about 5 to 32 seconds and resolved 43 more calls. Having the extra installed, for example through [all], changes nothing on its own: the graph and the build time stay the same. CRG_DISABLE_ENRICHMENT=1 keeps it off even when asked for. With it on, update scans only the Python files it re-parses, plus their dependents, whose edges it rewrites, and a target with no node in the graph is never written.
  • get_knowledge_gaps_tool now reports import_cycles: groups of files that import each other, with one loop written out and its import lines. load_time says whether the loop can fail as the files load; false means every loop has an import that runs late: inside a function, a dynamic import() or a Ruby autoload. To tell these apart, import edges now record extra.import_context: type_only, deferred or parent_package. Run code-review-graph build once to get it on an existing graph. Part of #434.
  • query_graph_tool has a new path_to pattern that answers how one symbol or file reaches another: pattern="path_to", target="A", to="B", or code-review-graph query path_to A --to B. Each hop says how sure it is, and the answer names the weakest hop. A longer route through bound edges beats a shorter one that guesses at a name.
  • code-review-graph install --steering adds an opt-in Claude Code hook that puts graph context beside each search and read. enrich has existed for a long time, but install never wrote the hook that runs it. It is off by default because it changes what the model sees on every such call. --no-steering removes only that hook, and uninstall removes it too. A failed lookup never stops the tool call, and a broken graph or odd input now leaves enrich silent.
  • serve --http --multi-repo runs one HTTP server for every repository in the registry, so the embedding model is loaded once (#607). Each tool call must name a registered repository in repo_root. A call with no repo_root, a relative path or a path that is not registered is refused with a clear message. Before, a shared HTTP server quietly answered such a call from --repo or its own working directory, which could be a repository the client never named. The registry is read on every call, so register and unregister take effect without a restart. The flag needs --http and cannot be combined with --repo, --auto-watch, --multi-worktree or CRG_DATA_DIR; the daemon still watches several repositories. stdio and plain serve --http are unchanged.
  • An opt-in local log of context savings, and a context-history command to read it (#813). Set CRG_CONTEXT_HISTORY=1 and every response that carries a context_savings estimate (the impact, review-context, detect-changes and compact architecture tools, and the detect-changes, update --brief, impact and architecture commands) adds one JSON line to context-history.jsonl next to graph.db: the time, the tool, the estimated full, graph and saved tokens, and where the call has them detail_level, the changed-file count and the depth. No file names, paths, symbol names or source text are written, nothing is sent anywhere, and the log rolls over at 1 MiB, so it never takes much more than 2 MiB. code-review-graph context-history prints it per UTC day or, with --by tool, per tool, with --days N and --json. Off by default; nothing is written in read-only mode.
  • The GitHub Action has an opt-in details input that adds graph context to the review comment (#882). It takes a list of sections: risk shows each term of the top symbols' risk scores, so a reader sees why a score moved and not only that it did; communities, hubs and coupling show the communities the change touches, its most connected symbols and the links that cross a community or language boundary; impact shows its impact radius and direct callers; gaps ranks the test gaps by what depends on them; graph gives the graph's size, community count, the method that found the communities (Leiden or file-based grouping), and versions. The block is folded away, shows at most 5 rows per list, is capped at 12,000 bytes with a note saying what was cut, and never takes room from the report. Left empty, the default, the Action runs the same commands and posts the same comment byte for byte. The data comes from the new code-review-graph detect-changes --details <sections> option, which adds a details object to the JSON and nothing else. Building communities now also records the method used, in graph metadata.
  • Symlinked folders can be followed, as an opt-in (#269). Pass --follow-symlinks to build, update or watch, or set CRG_FOLLOW_SYMLINKS=1; it is off by default, so nothing changes unless you ask. A project folder of links to code kept elsewhere (inputs/app -> ~/src/app) now builds a graph instead of an empty one. Links to folders outside the repository are followed too, because you made the link and asked for it, and their files are stored under the link's path inside the repository, never the outside path. A file inside the repository keeps its own path, so a link to a folder the graph already holds adds no copies. A link to a folder that holds it, or holds the repository, is skipped and logged, and each real folder is read once, so links cannot loop. At most CRG_MAX_FOLLOWED_FILES files (default 10,000) are taken, and a warning says when the cap is reached. update and watch add and drop these files as links and the files behind them come and go, matching a full build; watch also watches each followed folder outside the repository. Search, review context, flows, rename and forget work on these files through their in-repo path, and imports between them point there too. Any other path outside the repository is still refused, and the review context and flow source snippets no longer read one either. Symlinked files are still never followed.
  • Python modules that replace themselves in sys.modules are followed to the module they install. A compatibility shim such as sys.modules[__name__] = importlib.import_module("app.services.billing") hands importers app/services/billing.py, but the graph stopped at the shim: import billing targeted the shim file, billing.charge(...) and the TESTED_BY edge from a test calling it stayed unresolved because charge is not defined in the shim, and from billing import charge named a node that does not exist. The import edge, module-receiver calls and from shim import name now resolve to the real module, through chains of shims and through re-exports to the defining file. Only the literal, module-level form is followed; a computed or relative module name, a swap inside a block, or a target outside the repository leaves the file as the module. Because importers now depend on the real module, editing only the shim does not re-parse them on update; run build after re-pointing a shim.
  • semantic_search_nodes_tool can return the code with the hits. include_source=True attaches each hit's source lines, and expand_neighbors=True also attaches the direct callers and callees of the top hits with their source. max_source_lines caps each snippet, one line budget caps the whole response, cut-off snippets are marked source_truncated, and a stored path that resolves outside the repository is never read. Source is not attached when detail_level="minimal". Without this an agent got names and line numbers and opened the same files by hand, so the search cost tokens on top of the reads instead of replacing them.
  • The README and docs/REPRODUCING.md publish the first canonical agent_baseline capture (2026-09-22): against a grep-and-read-top-3-files baseline, the graph answer is a median 6.65x smaller across 18 questions on 6 repositories (range 4.2x to 72.6x). It predates the test-file ranking below, so a run today gives different numbers until it is redone. The per-repo CSVs are in evaluate/results/; the 2026-05-25 impact_accuracy CSVs are removed, as the 2026-08-02 captures replace them.
  • Codex installs a global CRG skill with MCP-first guidance and a read-only CLI fallback (#841).
  • install also writes post-checkout, post-merge and post-rewrite git hooks that run update, so the graph follows a branch switch, a merge or a git pull (merge or rebase mode) instead of waiting for the next commit. post-checkout runs on branch checkouts only and skips the checkout a rebase makes before replaying local commits; post-rewrite updates once the rebase has finished and ignores --amend, which pre-commit already covers. A rebase with nothing to replay never runs post-rewrite, so it updates at that checkout. An existing hook in a language other than shell is left alone. Each block is marked, appended to an existing hook, skipped in linked worktrees unless CRG_HOOK_WORKTREES=1, and removed by uninstall. --no-hooks skips them along with pre-commit.
  • Oracle PL/SQL files get structural parsing: packages, procedures, functions and the calls between them (#785).
  • Robot Framework .robot and .resource files are indexed (#967, #963).
  • Static Laravel routes produce Endpoint nodes and HANDLES edges at parse time (#1072, part of #851).
  • Spring AOP advice reaches its targets through @annotation and execution pointcuts (#916, #592).
  • serve has an opt-in mode for parallel graph access across Git worktrees (#1063).
  • Read-only MCP tools carry readOnlyHint, so clients can skip the approval prompt (#1124, #1123).
  • The troubleshooting guide explains the local-checkout workaround for Zed SSH projects (#1130).
  • .json files are indexed; before, they gave no nodes at all (part of #436). Each key and array shape becomes a JsonPath node joined by CONTAINS edges, in the same value-free shape as generic YAML: array items share one [*] path, and values never enter the graph. Comments and trailing commas, as in tsconfig.json, are fine. JSON is often data, so a lot stays out: translation files keep only a file node by the rule YAML uses (#1150), and so do test files, files under docs, examples, samples, demo or data folders, and files over 256 KB. A file stops at 1,000 paths, and an object with more than 200 keys shares one .* path. package-lock.json, npm-shrinkwrap.json, *.lock.json, project.assets.json and *.min.json are ignored. On vega, 473 JSON files add 1,228 paths, mostly its 37 package.json files; without the folder rule, docs/ alone would have added 12,725. Search ranks JSON below code unless you ask for it with kind="JsonPath", a $ path or a .json name, and JSON rows no longer take the places code needs: six translation.json files had pushed appWithTranslation out of the hits for "translation" on next-i18next. detect_changes_tool reports changed_json_paths. A JSON or YAML key escaped to a lone surrogate, such as "\ud800", is left out; before, a YAML one stopped the whole build. Run build to add JSON to a graph you already have.
  • MiMo Code is a supported platform (#593, PR #688 by @Aditya-Tripuraneni). install --platform mimo adds the server under mcp in .mimocode/mimocode.jsonc if that file exists, otherwise in .mimocode/mimocode.json, and writes the graph instructions to AGENTS.md. Other servers, settings and comments are kept. MiMo reads both files, so uninstall removes the entry from both.

Changed

  • Dependency minimums are raised to versions that are tested and that install on every supported Python: mcp>=1.29.0, anyio>=4.8.0, tree-sitter>=0.26.0, tree-sitter-language-pack>=0.13.0 and pyyaml>=6.0.2. PyYAML 6.0 fails to build on Python 3.12 and newer, and mcp 1.29 and fastmcp 3.2.4 already need a newer anyio than the old minimum of 4.0. Ranges stay ranges; pin exact versions in your own lockfile if you need them. (#896, #265)
  • staging is no longer promoted to testing automatically. The Auto promote workflow and its script are gone, so a person reviews and merges every promotion, and a test fails if any workflow merges a pull request.
  • Generic .yml and .yaml files are indexed as YamlPath nodes that hold key paths and value types, never values. detect_changes reports them as changed_yaml_paths, bounded by max_results; the analysis behind it takes at most CRG_MAX_CHANGED_YAML_PATHS (200) YAML paths, and as many JSON paths. pnpm-lock.yaml is ignored at any depth.
  • Search ranks test code below production code unless the query asks about tests (kind="Test", or a word such as "test", "spec", "fixture" or "mock"). A two-line test stub that names a symbol used to score as well as the implementation and could outrank it.
  • The installed instruction block tells agents to answer from include_source=True, expand_neighbors=True and open a file only when a snippet is cut off. It still says not to change code from graph output alone. Earlier blocks stay recognised for upgrade and uninstall.
  • The MCP server instructions now route by question type instead of introducing the server as a code-review tool. With deferred tool loading an agent sees only the tool names and these instructions, so "for code reviews" kept it on Grep for callers, importers and covering tests everywhere else. The new text maps each relationship question to its tool and leaves plain-text search to Grep. Measured with Claude Code on one TypeScript monorepo: across 8 ordinary tasks that never mention the graph (implement, review, brainstorm, plan), the graph was used in 1 run before and 6 after; plain-text questions stayed on Grep (0 of 4 moved). tests/test_server_instructions.py keeps every tool and query pattern the text names registered.
  • Stored vectors are scored with numpy when it is installed, about 20 times faster, and numpy stays optional (#1090, #1089).
  • CRG_VECTOR_CACHE=1 keeps the decoded embedding vectors in memory between searches instead of reading them all back from SQLite on every query. A search over 42,000 vectors of 4,096 dimensions goes from 2.3 s to 0.40 s once the cache is warm, with the same ranking. It is off by default because it costs RAM and holds a read connection open for the life of the process, and on Windows that keeps graph.db from being deleted or replaced.
  • find_dead_code collects its edge evidence once, not once per function (#1082, #982).
  • Plain C headers skip the C++ parse through a prefilter (#876, #802).
  • The dev dependency range allows pytest 9 (#1140).
  • Builds and updates use less memory and fewer database commits. The MCP server loads its tool modules on first use, so an idle server holds about 89 MB instead of 144 MB; new flows, flow members and summary rows are written in one transaction each; the files that depend on a changed file come from one query; and community detection no longer decodes every edge's metadata. The graph itself is unchanged (#1139).
  • fastmcp 4 is allowed (fastmcp>=3.2.4,<5) and uv.lock pins 4.1.6 (#970). fastmcp 4 brings the mcp SDK 2.x, so the server now answers server/discover and serves clients on the 2026-07-28 protocol, which issue #919 found it could not do. Older clients still use the initialize handshake as before. The prompts import Message from fastmcp.prompts, which works on fastmcp 3 and 4; the old fastmcp.prompts.prompt path is gone in 4 and stopped the server from starting.
  • The build-graph, review-delta and review-pr skills are written from one source, DOCUMENTARY_SKILLS in skills.py. scripts/generate_documentary_skills.py rewrites them, and a test fails if a file is edited by hand (#822).
  • install --gitignore adds the files and folders that run created to a marked block in the repository's .gitignore (#471). It is off by default, because .claude/, .mcp.json and CLAUDE.md can be team files. Only paths that did not exist before the run and that git does not track are listed, so a file you wrote or committed is never ignored. A path some rule already ignores is not repeated. Running install again merges into the same block, --dry-run shows the lines it would add, and uninstall removes the block and nothing else, so .gitignore comes back byte for byte. The .code-review-graph/ entry keeps its own handling.

Fixed

  • A fresh clone, set up as CONTRIBUTING.md says, now passes and stays clean:
    • Installing for any platform inside this checkout no longer leaves untracked files. The root .gitignore now lists what Cursor, Windsurf, Gemini CLI, Kiro, MiMo, OpenCode, CodeBuddy and Copilot write there, and tests/test_install_outputs_are_ignored.py fails if a platform starts writing a new path.
    • tests/test_token_budget.py no longer fails when the temp folder has a long path (the macOS default, or a long --basetemp). Answers hold absolute paths, so each one is now measured as if the test repo lived at the path CI gives it. No budget changed.
    • One parser test failed on Python 3.14, which uv sync picks when it is the newest Python installed. Python 3.14 parses the long expression the test used to break ast.parse, so the test now forces the failure on any Python that accepts it. The rest of the suite already passed on 3.14.
  • The release gate's corpus check expects this release's numbers. Its recorded baselines dated from before the new YAML, JSON, HTML and Liquid support, Ruby and Java test finding, and the C# and nested-scope identity changes, so 3 of its 8 repositories reported moves that were on purpose. The time budgets are unchanged.
  • The determinism check reorders the file list again. Since build started reading the file list through a new function, the check's reorder never reached it, so the three file-order tests built the same order twice: two passed for no reason and the known node-id bug looked fixed, which failed the release gate. The check now reorders the function build uses, and fails if a reordered build never read the reordered list.
  • The docs match the code again. docs/, the reference get_docs_section_tool serves, CONTRIBUTING.md, SECURITY.md, AGENTS.md, the Copilot instructions, two skills and the VS Code README now cover what the last two release trains added: all 19 query patterns, the search source options, the write lock and exit 75, the new git and steering hooks, untracked files, Liquid and the other new node kinds, confidence tiers and schema v15. They also drop claims the code no longer backs.
  • The read-only query script in the bundled skill (crg_readonly.py) now takes path_to with --to, so it offers every pattern the CLI query command accepts. It stops before running the CLI when path_to has no --to. A test now fails if the script's list drifts from tools/query.py or the CLI again; the patterns the CLI does not take yet are listed there with the reason.
  • The VS Code codeReviewGraph.cliPath setting no longer talks about a bundled version. The extension does not include the CLI; left empty, it runs code-review-graph from your PATH (#369).
  • install --dry-run shows what each file would become, and install writes nothing until you have answered its question (#173). The dry run used to list file names only, and a real install wrote the MCP config before it asked about CLAUDE.md and the other instruction files. Every install write (MCP configs, .gitignore, skills, hooks, git hooks, the OpenCode plugin, the instruction files, the --steering hook and the --gitignore block) now goes into a plan first. --dry-run prints that plan as one unified diff per file, a new file as its full content, cut at 60 lines a file with a note saying how many lines were left out, and writes nothing. A real install lists the files it will change, asks about the instruction files, and only then writes exactly the plan it showed. Ctrl-C at the question stops the install with nothing written, and a file whose content or permissions change while the question is open is left alone and reported. A file reached by two names, such as CLAUDE.md linked to AGENTS.md, is planned and written once. Commented JSONC configs and hook settings are previewed and written through the same splice, so their comments are kept. A hook settings file install will leave alone is named in the dry run too. Saying no to the question also keeps the instruction files out of the --gitignore block.
  • query_graph_tool no longer answers "ambiguous" when a full name has one real symbol. The name search splits on underscores, so hybrid_search also matched every test_hybrid_search_* and came back with 34 candidates and no answer, at about 5,200 tokens even with detail_level="minimal". A full name now means that name: a test or test helper with the same name gives way to the real symbol, and the summary says how many it skipped. Two real symbols with the name are still ambiguous. The same query now costs about 450 tokens and answers it. An ambiguous answer at minimal is five short rows sent once.
  • callees_of lists calls bound to a real node before bare-name guesses, so a few len calls can no longer fill the window and hide the real callee.
  • install no longer wipes a hook settings file it cannot read as strict JSON. One comment or trailing comma in .claude/settings.json, .qoder/settings.json, .codebuddy/settings.json, .gemini/settings.json, ~/.codex/hooks.json or ~/.cursor/hooks.json made the reader fail, and the file was overwritten with nothing but our hooks and no backup, so every setting in it was lost. These files are now read as JSONC, only what changed is written into a commented file, and every comment stays with the setting it describes. A file that cannot be merged, such as broken JSON or a hooks setting of the wrong shape, is left byte for byte as it was and named in the output, and install no longer says it installed those hooks. So is a file with a /* comment that never closes, where the hooks would have landed inside the comment and never run. A refused Gemini CLI install no longer rewrites the hook scripts in .gemini/hooks/. Cursor's file now gets a .bak like the others.
  • Adding a member to a commented JSONC config, as install does for an MCP entry, no longer moves a trailing comment onto the new member or drops a comment from an empty object.
  • .cjs files are indexed as JavaScript and .kts files as Kotlin, as .mjs and .kt already were. Every build skipped them, so their functions, imports and calls were missing from the graph and read as if they did not exist.
  • crg-daemon stop clears the health and state files of watchers that were killed outright. A killed watcher cannot clear its own, and on Windows that is every watcher, because stop ends the daemon with TerminateProcess and the Job Object takes the children with it. The file left behind read as a stalled watcher forever. stop also now stops any live watcher when it finds the daemon already gone. Health files for watchers the daemon never ran are left alone.
  • Graphs no longer hold data nothing reads (#1059). Schema v15 drops community_summaries, flow_snapshots and risk_index, which every full postprocess rewrote and nothing read, and whose rows kept pointing at deleted nodes. It also drops idx_nodes_qualified, idx_edges_source and idx_edges_target, as other indexes cover them. A new graph of this repository is 17% smaller, 252 MB down to 210 MB, with the same answers at the same speed. An upgraded graph keeps its file size and reuses the freed space for later writes; to shrink the file, delete it and rebuild, or run VACUUM. Older releases refuse a v15 graph, as with any schema change. Build results no longer carry summaries_computed or the summaries_s timing.
  • find_large_functions_tool, semantic_search_nodes_tool and traverse_graph_tool now have hard ceilings. At a huge limit or token_budget they returned up to 1.8M tokens on this repository, and now return under 25,000. limit and token_budget reject zero, negatives and booleans. The ceilings also bound the work: search ranks three times what it shows, the large-functions query fetches only what it shows and counts the rest in SQL, and the walk stops when the budget ends. find_large_functions_tool gains detail_level, and minimal costs less than standard. result_count on these tools now means the count before the cut, as on query_graph_tool. (#895, #888)
  • CRG_MAX_IMPACT_NODES now reaches get_impact_radius_tool. A literal default of 500 shadowed it, so the setting did nothing through MCP. (#888)
  • traverse_graph_tool in dfs mode no longer misses what lies past a node it first reached at the depth limit, then reached again by a shorter path.
  • Imports through an npm alias resolve to the local package (#343, PR #880). With "sharedLib": "npm:@scope/shared@^1.0.0" in an app's package.json, import x from 'sharedLib/some/module' was stored as a bare string, so importers_of and the impact radius found none of the apps that use the shared package. Now, when one package in the repository is named @scope/shared, the import reaches its file. Entry points are read as for a workspace member (exports, then source, module and main, with dist/ tried under src/). A wrong edge is worse than a missing one, so the import stays bare when the nearest package.json that names the dependency gives it an ordinary version, when the local package's version is outside the alias range, when two local packages share the name, when that package.json cannot be read, or when the package or file is one the build ignores. tsconfig paths still win. An alias also wins over a workspace member with the same name, because npm installs the aliased package under it. update does not re-parse importers when only a package.json changes; run build after changing an alias.
  • A file added by update now gets a community, as it does after build. It was in no community, so the update saw nothing affected, skipped community detection and left the file's nodes without one until the next full build (#1058, PR #1122 by @jay23606).
  • On Windows, build and update started with stdin on the NUL device (subprocess.DEVNULL, Node's stdio: "ignore") parse with threads. NUL passes isatty(), so they used a process pool, and a caller that stopped the run, such as a pre-commit hook timeout, left up to 8 worker processes behind each time. Only a real console now keeps the process pool (#1163, PR #1166 by @xOscarCodes).
  • The impact-accuracy benchmark's co-change mode no longer reports predicted_files = 0 on every commit (PR #823). git diff gives repo-relative paths and the graph stores absolute ones under the resolved root, so the seed never matched a node. The graph-derived ground truth now counts real neighbour files too, and seed_file stays relative to the repository.
  • Members of nested classes keep the full scope chain in every language that did not already (#934): in Java, Kotlin, Swift, Scala and others, Alpha.Same.run and Beta.Same.run are two nodes instead of one, a nested class is contained by its enclosing class, and three levels keep all three. Local calls stay inside their own class, and a JVM import of a nested class (import app.Outer.Inner) targets Outer.Inner. On gson this leaves no dangling CONTAINS edges (700 before) and 14 dangling CALLS (154 before). Existing graphs reparse their files with nested classes, and the callers of those members, once on the next update.
  • C# declarations no longer collapse across namespaces, and calls no longer bind to a type the caller cannot name. Qualified names now carry the namespace and the complete containing-type path (/repo/Handlers.cs::App.Report.ExportHandler.Run), and a dedicated namespace-aware pass binds each call from its own lexical scope: using directives are read at the byte offset of the namespace body that declares them, so a reopened or file-scoped body cannot leak its imports elsewhere; global:: and namespace aliases keep their meaning; Alias::Type searches only namespace aliases; and an unqualified call reaches a static method in an enclosing type without crossing a nearer method group. File co-location and short-name uniqueness are no longer treated as visibility evidence, so an ambiguous or unsupported reference stays unresolved instead of pointing at the wrong declaration. Overload selection, generic binding (#943), inherited members and using static remain outside this change (#946, with the nested-type identity needed by #934 and #937).
  • C# bindings are now re-evaluated on every update rather than fixed at first parse. Each call keeps its raw reference, so a binding is revoked when the usings or declarations behind it change, TESTED_BY mirrors move with their call, and deleting a callee returns incoming calls to raw references so recreating the declaration binds them again. A binding change marks flows dirty and the next full postprocess retraces them, which keeps callers and entry points outside the parsed files correct (#946).
  • C# generic declarations are keyed by arity, so a constructed receiver reaches the declaration it names — I<int> binds to I<T>, I binds to the separate I, and Pair<int> binds to neither when only Pair<K, V> is declared. The receiver's spelling is retained on the call and the arity is read from the syntax, so nested arguments and tuples count correctly. Type argument substitution, constraints and overload selection remain out of scope (#946).
  • Existing C# graphs reparse once on the next incremental update to adopt the new identities; a file that fails to parse is retried on its own instead of holding the version gate open and repeating a full rebuild (#944). Graphs that carry embeddings should re-run code-review-graph embed afterwards: the renamed C# nodes leave their previous vectors orphaned until a refresh purges them.
  • Unresolved C# member-call targets retain the receiver spelling (for example, Derived::Shared instead of Shared), so callers_of can return fewer matches for unsupported bindings, including inherited members and receiver types missing from the index (#946).
  • A file inside a Python package no longer answers an absolute standard library import. import json and import typing in Flask's own files were linked to flask/json/__init__.py and flask/typing.py, which made a false dependency and a false import cycle. Python 3 does not look in a package's own folder, so those imports now stay external. A typing.py beside a script, or at the top of a src/ folder, can still answer them.
  • A multi-clause Elixir function is now one node that spans all its clauses. The graph keeps one node per name, so the last clause used to overwrite the others: a change to an earlier clause found no function at all. parse_bytes now merges them, from the first clause's first line to the last clause's last line, and keeps the first clause's parameters. Clauses of different arities share the node too, as the graph has no arity in its names, so another function can sit inside the span (#979).
  • tests_for finds Java and Ruby tests. On spring-petclinic, production symbols with a test went from 5 to 61, and on sidekiq from 198 to 443. Run a full build to pick this up.
    • Java: a test in src/test/java can now reach src/main/java, for an import and for a class in its own package, which needs no import. this.field.x() and var x = new Cart() now give the type too.
    • Ruby: it "..." do, describe, before, let and test "..." do are test nodes in test files. A call is matched to a method only when the file says what class it is made on: a constant, a constructor, described_class, or a name assigned from one of those. A constant is looked up in the modules around it first, as Ruby does, so Cart inside module Shop is Shop::Cart.
    • A call in a test on something of unknown type no longer matches a method by name alone, in either language. An inherited method is not linked yet, so owner.setId() does not count for BaseEntity.setId.
    • By name, CartTests, CartTest, CartIT and CartSpec now count for the class Cart. Free text in a test's title no longer counts, so it "tests json output" is not a test of json.
    • A Ruby or Java method called only by its tests stays a flow entry point, as it was when those calls did not bind.
  • forget, embed and watch now obey the write lock, as build, update and postprocess already did, so none of them can rewrite a graph that a build has only half written. forget and embed take it for the whole run and exit 75 if another writer holds it, saying that nothing was done; both take --wait. embed_graph_tool answers status: contended. watch takes it for each write and lets go between them, so a running watcher never stops a build. If the lock is held, a watcher keeps its changes and retries, and can still be stopped.
  • A call the graph could not bind no longer looks like one it did (#984). The parser wrote a file path in front of a name it could not check, so h.hashCode() became One.java::Helper.hashCode though Helper has no such method, self.shared() named the subclass when shared was inherited, and Vec::new() in a Cargo crate became src/lib.rs::new. None of these names a node, yet each looked like a real link. Now no edge keeps a qualified name that has no node: it goes back to the plain name, keeps the qualified one in extra.claimed_endpoint, and returns to it if that node appears. A guess like this only counts for a method of the class the call named. In Rust, a type from outside the crate stays as written (Vec::new), which also lets WalkBuilder::new reach the real method; inner::bar() on an inline module lands on it; and on macOS and Windows the type Glob is no longer read as the file glob.rs. On ripgrep, 729 invented call targets and 341 invented test links went to 0, and real call links rose from 4,064 to 4,296 with none lost. spring-petclinic went from 2 to 0 and gin had none; their real links did not change.
  • Every edge claimed confidence 1.0 whatever had happened to it. For CALLS, REFERENCES and TESTED_BY, confidence_tier is now EXTRACTED (1.0) when the source names a real node, INFERRED (0.8, or the resolver's own score) when a resolver chose it, AMBIGUOUS (0.4) when several nodes fit, and UNRESOLVED (0.2) when none does. On gin, 14,353 of 16,249 such edges had no node and still said 1.0. No schema change: the next build, or an update that changes a file, puts an existing graph right.
  • callers_of, callees_of and references_to say when part of an answer is only name matches, in the existing confidence field: "3 of 8 call sites match by name only and may be wrong; resolution='direct' keeps the confirmed ones".
  • tests_for no longer counts a test as covering a function because both call the same outside name. String::new() in a function and in a test linked the two, and so did the invented targets above: ripgrep's decompress.rs::add claimed 10 tests, none of which reaches it, which lowered its risk score.
  • A call to an inherited method through a subclass now finds the method (#984, building on the idea in #1036 by Anthony Ala). this.handle() in Leaf extends Alpha, $this->dispatch() in a PHP test case and pet.getName() on a Java Pet that inherits getName named no node, so callers_of and tests_for on the real method found nothing. The edge now goes to the ancestor that declares the method, as INFERRED, and its TESTED_BY mirror goes with it. A class is known by its file, not its short name, and a parent counts only when the file, its imports, its Java package or its PHP namespace says which class it is; where Python could pick another ancestor, or a parent is outside the graph, the call stays unresolved. An update that adds or removes the method checks the edge again, so it matches a rebuild. A Java or TypeScript receiver's declared type now picks the class even when that class needs no import, and a call on another object no longer lands on the caller's own method when nothing says it is that class. On spring-petclinic, calls bound through a subclass rose from 2 to 86, on a TypeScript DDD example from 0 to 18 and on its PHP twin from 97 to 134, with no new invented targets; 28 self-calls the old rule made up were dropped or moved to the right class.
  • The edit hook updates the graph of the repository that holds the edited file, not the one the session started in (#311). From a folder of sibling repos it updated nothing, from an outer repo it updated the outer graph and never the nested one, and from a main checkout it never updated a linked worktree's graph. The Claude Code, Qoder and CodeBuddy hooks (and the plugin hooks/hooks.json) now pass the event JSON to hook-update, which reads the file path from it. That repository is updated if it already has a graph or is the session's own repository, which is still built on first edit as before; an edit anywhere else on disk changes no graph, and a submodule with no graph of its own passes the edit to its superproject. Without a file path in the event, the hook uses the event's cwd as before. Upgrade the package before running install again: an older CLI has no hook-update, so its edit hook does nothing (it still never fails the edit). install replaces the hook earlier releases wrote, and uninstall removes it, including the v2.3.4 to v2.3.6 form it used to leave behind.
  • detect-changes and detect_changes_tool now report what a diff that only deletes code removed, and no longer miss it or blame the code around it. A hunk such as @@ -430,42 +429,0 @@ adds no lines, so it named only the line before the gap: deleting a whole function reported 0 changed functions and risk 0.00, with nothing about the callers it left behind, and when the gap sat right after the previous function, that untouched function was listed as changed. The old version of the file is now read: a function or class that lost some lines is changed, and one that is gone is listed in removed_symbols with the callers, subclasses, importers and tests that still use it, and named in the summary ("Removed but still used: format_filename (risk 0.65, by FileError.init, File.convert, ...)"). The code around the gap is never marked. A removed symbol that is still used breaks its callers, so it is scored like a change (the flows it was in are traced again) and raises the overall risk, which fail-on-risk reads; one nothing uses adds nothing. Deleting click's echo with 20 callers now scores 0.65, where changing it scores 0.40. The PR comment gets a "Removed symbols" table. Up to 100 files are read per report (CRG_MAX_DELETION_FILES), and the summary says when a file could not be read. A deleted file is still not covered. Thanks to @decrypt-mm for reporting the case in #1167.
  • TypeScript and JavaScript imports resolve against compilerOptions.baseUrl when no paths pattern matches them, as tsc does. A tsconfig that sets baseUrl without paths resolved no non-relative import at all: on a NestJS monorepo whose services import src/..., 1,849 import edges stayed bare strings, importers_of for one service found 14 of its 35 importers, and calls on its injected services fell back to name matching.
  • A seeded visualize view (--seed-symbol, --seed-file, --seed-flow) reads only the part of the graph around its seed (#1145). It used to load every node, one query per file, and the whole edge table before cutting the view down to --max-nodes, so a two-node view of a large graph cost as much as the whole graph. It now reads a few columns of each node, which name lookup needs, counts edges from the indexes, and loads only the edges that touch the view. On this repository's graph a one-symbol view went from 380 SQL statements to 20 and from 120 MB to 13 MB at peak, with the same output byte for byte. A path query still reads the whole graph, since a route can run anywhere, and so does a view that reaches most of a large graph.
  • Translation files no longer flood the graph. A translation file repeats the same keys once per language, and each key became a YamlPath node: on sidekiq, web/locales/*.yml gave 3,147 of the 3,336 YamlPath nodes. A YAML file in a locale, locales, i18n, l10n or translations folder whose name or sub-folder is a language code, such as config/locales/devise.en.yml or locales/pt-BR/app.yml, now gets a file node only. Sidekiq's graph falls from 5,085 nodes to 1,935 and its database from 45 MB to 31 MB. A name alone is not enough, so config/en.yml and .github/workflows/it.yml keep their paths, and so does locales/deploy.yml. Run build to clear these nodes from a graph you already have; update only re-reads files that changed.
  • semantic_search_nodes_tool and cross_repo_search_tool apply kind before they cut the hits, not after. Rows of another kind could fill the fetch limit, so asking for functions could return few or none when many existed: dead with kind="Function" found 1 of them on sidekiq and now finds 9. If the exact reading of a query matches only other kinds, the wider reading is now tried.
  • tests_for gives the same answer every time on the same graph. On a large class it follows at most 50 callees (CRG_MAX_TRANSITIVE_FRONTIER), and it picked them in an order that changed with each new process, so an MCP restart or a new CLI call gave a different list. On koel, 20 runs on one graph gave SongService anything from 43 to 90 tests, and every run gave a different list. The 50 are now the first by name, with named callees before bare ones, which are never followed. The detect_changes risk score counts the same tests, so it is steady too. Two builds of the same code already gave the same graph; only the query moved.
  • Imports of sibling packages in npm, yarn and pnpm workspaces resolve to the package's source files (#1030). A specifier such as @acme/shared or @acme/sdk/browser names a package-manager link, not a compilerOptions.paths alias, so it was stored bare and importers_of, the impact radius and references_to stopped at every package boundary. Calls through a package's barrel file now reach the defining file as well. The new WorkspaceResolver finds the nearest pnpm-workspace.yaml or package.json with workspaces inside the repository, maps each member's name to its directory, and reads exports (subpaths, conditions, fallback arrays and * patterns, with Node's key priority; a null entry or an unlisted subpath stays unresolved), then source, module, main, types and typings. A target under dist/, build/, out/ or lib/ is tried under src/ with source extensions first, because compiled output is normally absent from a checkout. tsconfig paths still win when both apply. Because a wrong edge is worse than a missing one, an unscoped name resolves only when the importing package declares it as a dependency, Node builtin names such as events never resolve to a workspace member, a name two members share resolves to neither, probes are case-exact on every platform, and a target the build would not index keeps the bare specifier.
  • The Claude Code plugin's hooks load again. hooks/hooks.json held a bare event map, which current Claude Code rejects with "hooks.json must have hooks (the hook matchers) or modules (hooks modules), or both", so the SessionStart and PostToolUse hooks never ran. The events now sit under a top-level "hooks" key, the shape the plugins reference documents. The hooks carry the same guards as the ones install writes, so a missing code-review-graph, a folder outside Git or a failed command shows no hook error, and the update hook matches Edit|Write only, so a Bash command can no longer start a full build in a repository with no graph.
  • The OpenCode installer now detects the installed OpenCode major version and writes the matching plugin API: returned hooks for OpenCode 1.x and the id + setup definition for OpenCode 2.x. Both templates use bounded node:child_process argv execution. The 1.x template runs from the OpenCode worktree/current directory instead of passing that directory as --repo, so starting OpenCode in a repository subdirectory still resolves the graph root. The 2.x template keeps its plugin package import type-only, so OpenCode 2.0.0–2.0.24 can load it without a runtime @opencode/plugin dependency. This fixes the V2 loader error Plugin must export a default definition with an id and an effect or setup function without dropping 1.x support (#1038, #947). Each plugin instance runs one graph update at a time, and edits made during a run share one follow-up run, because an overlapping update reads the first run's in-progress marker as a crashed build and does a full rebuild.
  • Codex hooks act on the repository named by the event's cwd instead of the directory Codex was launched from, so they cannot update the wrong graph. The generated hooks now run the new hook-update and hook-status commands, which ignore a missing, relative or non-repository cwd. Upgrade the package before running install --platform codex again; install and uninstall both recognise the hooks earlier releases wrote.
  • **/vendor/** applies only when a root manifest shows vendor/ holds dependencies (composer.json, go.mod, go.work, Gemfile, Rakefile, Cargo.toml or *.gemspec), and a nested one excludes its sibling vendor/. A source package named vendor in any other repository stays in the graph; add vendor/ to .code-review-graphignore to exclude it (#1056).
  • from module import name as alias followed by alias(...) is recorded as a call to module.py::name. It used to qualify the alias, module.py::alias, a target that matches no node.
  • A call through a Python module receiver whose module only re-exports the name (pkg.helper() where pkg/__init__.py does from .impl import helper) resolves to the defining file. The resolver required the name at the top level of the receiver's own module, so the call stayed unresolved.
  • _strip_plsql_noise tracked string-literal state per line and stripped /* */ comments over the raw text in a separate first pass, so a string literal spanning multiple lines (or containing an unterminated /*) could desynchronize the rest of the file: an END name;-shaped substring inside a multi-line string terminated a block early, an Oracle q'[...]'/Q'<...>' quoted string (which allows unescaped ' inside it) confused the plain '...' state machine the same way, and an unterminated /* inside one string literal could pair up with an unrelated */ inside a later string and blank out everything between them — including a whole CREATE PROCEDURE statement that happened to fall in that span, which also regressed plain (non-Oracle) SQL files. It now tracks string and comment state in one pass over the whole file. _plsql_block_end's terminator search also ran unbounded to end-of-file, so a package body member using a bare END; could match an unrelated, later package body's same-named member's END name; instead of its own, and the unbounded search was quadratic on a file with many members (~4s for 4,000). It now takes a limit, bounding a member's search to its own enclosing package body (PR #785 review).
  • An incremental update now keeps flows and communities in step with the graph. A new entry point gets its flow, a re-parsed file's flows follow its nodes to their new IDs, and a deleted file leaves no empty community behind. Before, relative changed paths never matched the stored absolute ones, so flows and communities were silently skipped (#569, #1058).
  • Overlapping graph writes no longer stack their work (#1073). build, update and postprocess take a per-repository flock on <data dir>/write.lock before parsing or rewriting derived data, so a second invocation reports status: contended (CLI: Skipped: another code-review-graph ... is already running for this repository (PID n)) and exits 75 (EX_TEMPFAIL) instead of repeating the work. Commit hooks, editor-save hooks and agent tool hooks fire in bursts, and until now the only cross-process protection was SQLite's busy timeout, which is not reached until the first write — every duplicate run paid for a complete parse first, and a postprocess could rewrite flows/communities/FTS for a graph another process was still storing. Ten concurrent update runs of one repository used to mean ten process pools on the same files. --wait (wait_for_lock) blocks until the in-flight write finishes, for callers that want the work done rather than skipped; two repositories never block each other; and the fixed lock path lets external tooling take the same lock. The lock covers schema initialization, legacy migration and CLI recovery too. Forked parse workers close inherited lock handles, so killing their parent releases the lock even while workers remain alive. A filesystem without flock support logs a warning and runs unlocked rather than reporting a competing writer. Invoke protected CRG commands directly; external flock wrappers are for other writers.
  • An explicit embedding refresh (--embedding-provider with --embedding-model, or the embedding_provider and embedding_model arguments of build_or_update_graph_tool and run_postprocess_tool) on a graph with no embeddings yet now reports that it embedded nothing. build, update and postprocess return embeddings_refresh_skipped: true and a warning to run code-review-graph embed once, and so does an update that finds no changes. watch logs the same warning after each update and keeps running; a refresh that fails against an existing index still stops it, as before. The refresh used to return ok with no embedding field, the same result as a build that never asked for one. It still loads no model and makes no network call in that case (#1070).
  • A rename rewrites the import statements that name the symbol (#1107). The preview looked for import edges that target the symbol, but Python import edges target the imported module's file, so from pkg.util import helper was never edited and the applied code raised ImportError. The preview now reads the defining module and every module that imports it, and follows Python's scope rules: from module import name in every spelling (several names on a line, parenthesized over several lines with comments, as aliases, relative imports), packages that re-export the name and their __all__, import module then module.name, and imports inside functions. Calls that no import statement names are still found through the graph's call edges and resolved the same way: pkg.util.name() after import pkg, sub.util.name() after from pkg import sub, and a module bound by importlib.import_module. JavaScript and TypeScript get the same for import { name }, import { name as x }, local export lists, export { name } from and export * from barrels (also a public barrel nothing in the repository imports from), and ns.name through import * as ns, import ns = require(), const ns = require(), await import() and import().then(). A CommonJS module.exports = { name } keeps its key, so its importers keep the old name. An alias keeps its own name; a parameter, local, comprehension variable or class attribute that reuses the name is left alone, and so are comments, strings and longer names that contain it. A call inside a shell "$(...)" is code and is renamed.
  • Rename edits are exact. Each one carries the column where the old name starts and the site it is (definition, call, reference, import, export, attribute, __all__), and applying replaces the name only there. Before, applying replaced the first substring match on the line (helper_count became double_count) and, when the line had none, the first match anywhere in the file. A method call chained over several lines, or made inside an f-string, is renamed where its name is rather than in a comment or the string's text. An edit that no longer matches is skipped and counted in edits_skipped. CRLF line endings, a byte-order mark and non-ASCII text survive, and a file that is not UTF-8 is no longer rewritten with replacement characters.
  • refactor_tool(mode="rename") resolves old_name exactly. It accepts a name, Class.method or a qualified name such as pkg/util.py::helper. When a bare name belongs to several symbols the first by file and line is renamed, and the preview's new qualified_name says which; pass a qualified name to pick another. A name that matches no symbol is not_found instead of falling back to a symbol whose name merely contains it.
  • tsconfig and jsconfig path aliases inherited through a relative extends resolve against the config that declares baseUrl (or paths, when no config in the chain sets baseUrl), as tsc does, instead of the nearest config's directory. In an Nx or Turborepo layout every app extending a root tsconfig.base.json lost its alias imports, so importers_of and the impact radius of a shared library missed those apps (#1110). Also as in tsc, a base config reached through a symlink (to the file or to a directory above it) anchors where the link sits, and a .. in its baseUrl or paths climbs from there; "baseUrl": null in an extending config unsets the inherited baseUrl.
  • Python @property reads produce edges, so callers_of and find_dead_code see them (#1065, #1060).
  • Python and Ruby classes that share a leaf name in one file no longer overwrite each other's methods (#1087, #999).
  • Terraform provider aliases keep distinct nodes, and references reach the right one (#1083, #985).
  • PowerShell functions, classes, methods and calls are extracted again (#1079, #1067).
  • Elixir def with a when guard is indexed and short-form bodies are walked (#980, part of #979).
  • JavaScript and TypeScript generator functions are Function nodes that own their calls (#1131).
  • Components wrapped in forwardRef, memo and similar calls are Function nodes (#972, #971).
  • TypeScript abstract classes are indexed, with nodes for their abstract methods (#1033, #1031).
  • Compact Ruby class Foo::Bar declarations emit Class nodes with stable identity, and all nested Ruby methods now carry their full scope, such as Auth.User.to_s. This moves the schema to version 14, and the next update re-keys Ruby files by itself (#933, #932).
  • install no longer appends a shell block to a pre-commit hook written in another language, which made every commit fail.
  • uninstall removes the empty Codex skills folder it created.
  • Calls inside Rust if false blocks are left out of the graph (#1132).
  • @Override methods and JUnit lifecycle methods count as entry points in dead-code analysis (#1035, #1034).
  • Spring calls on a concrete injected type stay on that type instead of moving to its only subclass (#1075, #1074).
  • tests_for and the test-gap report name tests reached through spec helpers (#1128, #1109).
  • get_impact_radius and get_review_context report unknown risk when a requested path is not in the graph (#1084, #983).
  • The real-absence claim is withdrawn while untracked source exists (#987).
  • Review guidance applies the lifecycle test-gap exemption (#871, #865).
  • A registered data directory that has gone missing no longer strands a local graph (#1080, #1057).
  • Untracked files reach build and update (#1094, #998).
  • A no-op update repairs stale FTS left by --skip-postprocess (#1105, #1104).
  • MCP auto-watch runs post-processing after updates (#1108, #1103) and stops and joins its watcher before closing the store (#1127, #1121).
  • Partial graphs recover once and then stay incremental (#881, #803).
  • Renames through re-exports match a full build, and importers keep their flows and communities (#1062).
  • Watch mode does not report a refused directory as adopted (#910, #907) and does not watch ignored trees created after startup (#973, #908).
  • Windows daemon logs accept Unicode and tolerate vanished PIDs (#1129, #866), and a Job Object ends watchers when the daemon dies (#958, part of #925).
  • tests_for names PHP tests that reach a class through the Laravel container, and says they are guesses (part of #850). Run a full build to pick this up.
    • A PHP call on an expression, such as $this->service->run() or app(Service::class)->run(), was bound to a method of the same name in the calling file. A test named after the method it tests was recorded as calling itself: 113 such edges from tests on koel/koel. It now stays unresolved, as the same call in Python does.
    • When more than one class the test imports defines the called method, the test is listed with inferred_by: "ambiguous_call", unless the call is made on one of those classes that the test builds, as in (new UpdateBooks)->handle(). Building one class does not settle the test's other calls. On koel, classes with a test file that names them but no tests_for answer went from 17 to 12.
    • The summary says how many rows are guesses, and detail_level="minimal" keeps inferred_by on tests_for rows.
    • A guess is never coverage: test gaps and risk scores do not count it. The detect_changes summary says PHP gaps are a guess, since container and HTTP calls are not traced (#851 is the full fix).
  • Migration up and down methods are not test gaps when the file is in a migrations or migrate folder inside the repository (#850).

Upgrade notes

  • Schema version: 13 → 15. Two migrations run by themselves the first time any command opens the graph. Both are quick, as neither rewrites a table:

    • v14 clears the stored hash of every Ruby file, so the next update parses those files again and gives Ruby classes and methods their full names, such as Auth.User.to_s. Until that update, Ruby rows keep their old names.
    • v15 drops community_summaries, flow_snapshots and risk_index, which nothing read, and three indexes that other indexes cover. The file keeps its size and reuses the space; to shrink it, delete it and run build, or run VACUUM.

    Rolling back needs a rebuild. Migrations only go forward. 2.3.9 refuses a v15 graph with "was written by a newer code-review-graph (schema v15; this build understands v13)", so to go back, delete .code-review-graph/graph.db and run code-review-graph build with the older release.

  • The VS Code extension has to be updated as well. 0.3.0 accepts v13 and answers a v15 database with "Database was created with a newer version (schema v15). Update the extension." This repository now carries 0.4.0, which accepts v15. The extension is published apart from the Python package, so until a 0.4.0 .vsix is built and installed, the Code Graph panel shows that error once the CLI has moved the graph to v15.

  • Run code-review-graph build once. update only parses files that changed, so most fixes in this release reach a file only when it is parsed again: the new file types (HTML, XSLT, Liquid, JSON, plain YAML, .cjs and .kts), translation files losing their key nodes, vendor/ folders that hold source, workspace and npm alias imports, tsconfig baseUrl and extends, extra.import_context on import edges, the call and test fixes (#984, inherited methods, tests_for for Java, Ruby and PHP) and the confidence tiers. Ruby files (v14 above), files with nested classes and C# files are the exception: the next update parses them again by itself. If the graph has embeddings, run code-review-graph embed after that, because renamed C# nodes leave their old vectors behind.

  • Upgrade the package, then run code-review-graph install again. The hooks and instruction blocks an earlier release wrote do not update themselves. Install now also writes post-checkout, post-merge and post-rewrite git hooks; the edit hook for Claude Code, Qoder and CodeBuddy, and the Codex hooks, call the new hook-update and hook-status commands; and the instruction block has new search guidance. Upgrade first: an older CLI has no hook-update, so the new edit hook would do nothing. Install and uninstall still recognise everything 2.3.9 and earlier releases wrote.

  • New settings are off by default. Nothing changes unless you turn them on: --enrich or CRG_ENABLE_ENRICHMENT=1 (needs the enrichment extra), install --steering, install --gitignore, --follow-symlinks or CRG_FOLLOW_SYMLINKS=1, CRG_CONTEXT_HISTORY=1, CRG_VECTOR_CACHE=1, serve --http --multi-repo, serve --multi-worktree and the Action's details input.

  • Output and dependency changes. Build results no longer carry summaries_computed or summaries_s. On find_large_functions_tool, semantic_search_nodes_tool and traverse_graph_tool, result_count is now the count before the cut. The lowest supported versions of mcp, anyio, tree-sitter, tree-sitter-language-pack and pyyaml are higher, and fastmcp 4 is allowed (see Changed).

  • GitHub Action cache. The cache key segment moves from schema13 to schema15, so the first run after upgrading restores no graph cache and builds from scratch. Self-hosted runners can delete the old code-review-graph-schema13-* entries.

mineru-4.2.1-releasedMinerU 4.2.1 Stable2026-11-11T13:53:12Z

4.2.1

  • 修复 Doclib 截断续读遗漏内容。Markdown 文档或单页读取在 context=0 时，达到长度限制后通过 next_request.locator 与 next_request.after 继续读取当前页的剩余内容，读完后再建议下一页。CLI 续读标记和 SDK 文档同步更新。
  • 完善托管解析服务的故障恢复。连续启动或重启失败达到上限后停止自动重试，并清理残留进程。重新设置托管模式或档位、重启文档库服务后可再次尝试恢复。切换模式、档位及关闭服务时，完善旧进程和控制通道的回收。
  • 修复 Windows 托管服务端口探测。按照服务实际绑定行为检查端口，在非严格端口模式下跳过已占用或被系统保留的端口，减少探测成功后仍无法启动的问题。
  • 改善文档库服务的优雅退出。通过宿主停机回调执行生命周期清理，使 Windows 关闭流程也能回收后台任务、托管解析进程和数据库资源。
  • 修复 Windows GPU 显存信息缺失。完善 DXGI 与硬件库存的 PCI 地址匹配，在同型号设备条目存在歧义时保留一致的显存和设备属性，提高硬件诊断信息的完整性。
  • 修复原生 PDF 正文缺失。接受因浮点误差接近 360° 的正常文本方向，避免水平文字在原生文本回填时被错误过滤。

集成提示。嵌入式 Doclib 宿主需要设置 shutdown 回调以支持关闭接口，缺少回调时返回 service_unavailable。标准 mineru server 入口已配置该回调。

4.2.1

  • Complete Doclib continuation after truncation. Markdown document and single-page reads with context=0 use both next_request.locator and next_request.after to resume the unfinished page after reaching the output limit, then suggest the next page. CLI continuation markers and SDK documentation follow the same contract.
  • Bound managed parse-server recovery. Stop automatic retries after repeated startup or restart failures and clean up remaining processes. Setting the managed mode or tier again, or restarting the document library server, allows recovery to resume. Improve process and control-channel cleanup during mode changes, tier changes, and shutdown.
  • Correct Windows managed-port probing. Match the server's actual binding behavior and skip occupied or system-reserved ports when strict-port mode is disabled, reducing startup failures after a successful port probe.
  • Graceful document library shutdown. Use a host shutdown callback to run lifecycle cleanup, including background tasks, managed parsing processes, and database resources on Windows.
  • Restore Windows GPU memory information. Improve PCI-address matching between DXGI and the hardware inventory, and retain consistent memory and device properties when same-model entries have ambiguous identities.
  • Preserve native PDF body text. Accept normal text directions close to 360° due to floating-point error, preventing horizontal text from being filtered during native text restoration.

Integration note. Embedded Doclib hosts must provide a shutdown callback to support the shutdown endpoint. A missing callback returns service_unavailable. The standard mineru server entry point already provides the callback.

升级 / Upgrade

pip install -U "mineru==4.2.1"

1.13.0 (2026-11-11)

  • config: accept config files with a UTF-8 BOM (#3365) (63170b1)
  • console: stop resolving a cyclic Error.cause chain after 10 causes (#3338) (172656a)

  • extensions: say list_extensions only lists unpacked extensions (#3433) (1383bb9)
  • ignore an invalid cached version in the update check (#3392) (3a9f378)
  • input: validate press_key and submitKey parameters in Zod schema (#3382) (375a269)
  • network: start an empty request list for navigations without a request (#3357) (b701776)
  • normalize non-Error values thrown by tool handlers (#3068) (535a3dc)
  • performance: error when performance_stop_trace is called on a non-recording page (#2971) (#3117) (1ab630a)
  • plugin: launch the Claude Code MCP server with npx --prefix (#3353) (a24334d)
  • preserve task serialization on request cancellation (#3079) (03fd4e8)
  • report page loads that keep the URL (#3395) (3cc38bb)
  • save binary request bodies byte for byte (#3404) (e3eb901)
  • suggest --headless when Chrome cannot find an X server (#3428) (8b0856a)
